## **Pointers for discussion:** 

1. What kind and number of training courses were provided by the SBCC cells to the trainers in the last five years? Please elucidate with examples covering the topics and themes covered in the training  (e.g., understanding of SBCC, facilitation and training skills, community mobilization, developing a training module and material and facilitating community-based communication activities. The themes for example may cover the issues related to RI, usage of clean water and sanitation and so on, depending on the state categorization). 

2. Could you please give an overview of the training process? Please emphasize the themes, pedagogy, method of training and overall experience. Please indicate if there was any support from the state, other district/s, technical expert/s, and other departmental functionaries in the district? 

3. How critical do you think that communication is to ensure behavior change to enhance uptake of flagship programmes and basic services around health, education, nutrition, child protection? 

UNICEF SBCC Cells Programme Evaluation for 2017-21 PwC 

125 

4. If there are any change in the ways/methods of generating awareness or facilitating change in behavior of the community or beneficiaries visible in the last five years, please explain 

5. What is the role of the trainers (all of you) in ensuring there is improved demand and uptake of services? 6. If any training or orientation on understanding of gender equality, equity and inclusion in BCC have been organized for trainers? 

7. Was there any structured exercise for understanding the training and capacity building needs of trainers? The average number of training courses undertaken by this group annually in the last five years on SBCC for the trainees at various levels. 

8. Has the training support and skill development initiatives from the SBCC cell enhanced the quality of work and understanding on the importance of social and behavior change communication of those interacted with at the district level and below. If yes, please explain by sharing examples of such experiences in which it has helped you to execute training for the frontline workers and local functionaries for improved demand for basic services? 

9. How are the TARANG modules used in the training process of trainees? Can you please share an overview of the broad components of the training? 

10. Is there any process in place to gather inputs and field insights taken of master trainers in developing BCC material, training modules, programme or strategy for the state and district? If yes, how? If not, share your views? 

11. What on-field support is made available to aid your training delivery? What additional support would help you? 

12. Does the training delivered by Master Trainers have a gender sensitive and social equity lens? How has this lens been incorporated in the delivery and content, especially in the last five years? 

13. Did Covid 19 influence any change in the training process? Was the training switched to an online mode? 

14. In your opinion how can the training content and delivery be strengthened? In your opinion how can the SBCC Cell be better supported in the strengthening of training content and delivery? 

## **FGD Guide for Trainees of SBCC Cells Introduction (Can be modified as per interviewer):** 

With this group facilitator/s may refer to the Department with which the SBCC Cell is associated with (in West Bengal we may refer to the District Administration too) and that they are here to assess certain aspects of the programme delivery and its effectiveness.  The facilitator can introduce themselves, “I, ______________, am from the evaluation team would like to discuss with you about your communication with local stakeholders particularly AWW/ ASHA and other local workers/leaders in increasing community’s demand and access to basic services such as health, WASH, nutrition, education, protection or otherwise. I am accompanied by__________________, my co-facilitator”. 

Thank the participants for joining the discussion and share that this is meant for collective discussion and not to capture individual views. Thus **, no personal names will be reflected in the report** .  It is also important to indicate that the FGD would take about one hour of their time. As a process, the facilitator will raise the issue of discussion and the participants would share their knowledge, views, perceptions, and thoughts. It is important to give the participants the confidence that **they can express their views without fear, and they would not be quoted or forced to participate at any point of time and their participation is entirely voluntary** . **Request permission to record the interaction for documentation purposes. All information and responses shared during the discussion would remain confidential and anonymous. All the evaluators would follow the ‘do no harm principle’ in conducting the discussion.** 

Before posing the specific questions, the facilitator allows participants to introduce themselves; particularly their role and department or programme they are associated with and for how long. 

## **Pointers for discussion:** 

1. Please share your thoughts on the necessity of communication and community mobilization with the community in providing services. (An ice breaking question) 

UNICEF SBCC Cells Programme Evaluation for 2017-21 PwC 

126 

2. Their exposure to nature of training that they have received from district/ circle/ block level on SBCC (facilitator should explain what SBCC all about). Allow the participants to recall the various training received in the last five years. Share the number and themes under each year, as per recall 

3. What are the themes and topics that are covered in the discussions conducted with trainers (Probe on topics covered in the dissemination meetings, e.g., home visits, community education, interpersonal communication, conducting meetings, other community mobilization techniques and on themes related ANC, immunization, COVID related issues, nutrition, childcare, child marriage and so on) 

4. Discuss if you feel the training that you received was relevant to the issues and challenges in supporting community access services available? Please probe specifically on issues related to discrimination and practices due to gender (men/ women/boys/ girls/others), income (poor and marginalize), social groups (SC. ST and others) and inclusion of people with disability on access to services. 

5. How often do you meet the target group and disseminate the message? 

   - a. Probe on how the target group is identified and prioritized. (People and groups with local access to services, marginalized groups, women, mother in laws, local leaders, and other age groups and so on). 

   - b. Probe on key messages they deliver from the point of view of if they understand the criticality of the key issues being addressed through the messages (Probe areas on existing knowledge and understanding, and the approach of trainees in delivering information, and their own biases and practices, their attitude to behavioral change such as biases around gender inequality and domestic violence) 

6. Indicate if there has been any change in communicating or interacting with the community before five years and now (specifically for the ones in the system for more than five years). Share examples of the same 

Are there community groups who you communicate with on a regular basis and the ones that you find challenging to share messages? 

7. Are you provided with any aid and materials to support your communication with the community and the beneficiaries? (Probe if they are also trained to prepare the communication materials such as posters, flip books themselves) 

8. How aligned do you think the training programmes and job aids/ materials provided are to your capabilities, skill sets, knowledge, and ability to deliver services? In this context enquire if any training that needs assessment for communication has been done. 

9. How receptive are your target groups to the messages shared through meetings or other mechanisms? _(Probe on target groups – women, men, couples,_ marginalized/vulnerable groups _)_ Who are the target priority groups for such meetings/gatherings (if there are any specifics to be mentioned)? 

10. Share your insights on if and how community mobilization and awareness programmes have enabled identifying and addressing the challenges to increase demand for RI, health services, nutrition programs and WASH services. Has this impacted the availability and accessibility of such services in your area? 

11. Could you share the challenges that you face in your day-to-day work and training? (For instance: community’s unwillingness to attend meetings, group sessions, training, etc., resistance from communities’ in talking with you on certain topic etc.) Please probe if challenges are around infrastructure or lack of relevant materials, human resources, deep rooted culture/ tradition, or biases and so on 

12. Is there any supervision or guidance while executing the communication activities? In case there is, how and what is the nature of this supervision or quality assurance 

13. In your opinion, who are the key influencers that support and increase possibilities of community mobilization? 

14. Please share if you have received any feedback from the target group on the effectiveness of key community mobilization activities done by you. Have target beneficiaries acknowledged any positive change in their behavior which resulted from your inputs? (Probe if this has increased participation in meetings, adoption of positive behavior around health practices, social biases etc.). 

15. Share your insights on the support mechanisms received (if any) from the government   in improving the effectiveness and efficiency of Covid response? (Probe for support received to improve access to basic support services including vaccine roll-out?) 

UNICEF SBCC Cells Programme Evaluation for 2017-21 PwC 

127 

16. Do you think that there are any specific facilities that you would need as trainees to make the training received more relevant to specific community-based contexts? For instance: what are the relevant tools and IEC materials received and used to deliver training and activities in the community? 

17. Please share your thoughts and insights on the following: 

   - a. Identification of priority messages, target groups and delivery of the message through the various training content received 

   - c. Inputs received to support an environment where communities and key influencers are engaged in developing and designing messages on health and other aspects through various platforms.? 

## **FGD with Community members Introduction (Can be modified as per facilitator):** 

With this group, facilitator/s may refer to the Department with which the SBCC Cell is associated with (in case of West Bengal we may refer to the District Administration too) and that they are here to understand what has worked and not worked with respect to communication related intervention/s.  The facilitator can introduce themselves, “I, ______________, am from the evaluation team and would like to discuss with you about your communication with local stakeholders particularly AWW/ ASHA and other local workers/leaders in increasing community’s demand and access to basic services such as health, WASH, nutrition, education, or others. I am accompanied by__________________, my co-facilitator”. 

Thank the participants for joining the discussion and share that this is meant for collective discussion and not to capture individual views. Thus **, no personal names will be reflected in the report** .  It is also important to indicate that the FGD would take about one hour of their time. As a process, the facilitator will raise the issue of discussion and the participants would share their knowledge, views, perceptions, and thoughts. It is important to give the participants the confidence that **they can express their views without fear, and they would not be quoted or forced to participate at any point of time and their participation is entirely voluntary** . **Request permission to record the interaction for documentation purposes. All information and responses shared during the discussion would remain confidential and anonymous. All the evaluators would follow the ‘do no harm principle’ in conducting the discussion.** 

## **Pointers for discussion:** 

1. Have any kind of campaigns/ meetings/sessions on health issues, vaccination, WASH, nutrition, education etc. happened in your village? What were these? Can I give you some details? Who conducted these meetings/campaigns? (Probe for interactions with refer to examples such as AWW/ ASHA, local leaders or SHG members) 

2. Are there any specific days or occasion when you have interactions with the FLWs (ASHA, AWWs, etc.) (e.g., during nutrition days, immunization week, mothers meeting at Anganwadi, home visits, workshops, or others) 

(Probe on any other ways that they have access to the messages related to the above-mentioned sectors such as street plays, group meetings, gram sabhas, local TV channel, mobile messages, posters SHG meetings and so on). 

3. Please share the specific themes and messages that they cover in these sessions 

4. Please indicate the most covered themes (Probe on social issues, behavioral change around health and hygiene, prejudices against gender, specific groups such as economically disadvantaged, marginalized groups, etc.) Ensure that facilitator allows participants to add more to the list and cover as many topics and themes as they can recall. 

5. Which groups/ target groups in your community do they mostly target (mothers, women, men, local community). If there is a trend in the last five years to focus on a certain group or community. 

6. Your views on the alignment of the topics discussed or communicated with the lives and experiences of the community (please refer to local priorities, the COVID situation as an example). Please probe into the usefulness of the top is in the community’s context. 

UNICEF SBCC Cells Programme Evaluation for 2017-21 PwC 

128 

7. If there was any scope to share your/ community’s thoughts on how and what messages and themes to cover as a part of discussions or communication activities. (wrt. The communication with the representatives of the government, workers, frontline functionaries from various departments) 

8. Do you think these messages have brought about any change in the community’s use/ uptake for the government services (please refer to services linked to the state categorization, immunization, other pre- and post-natal services, nutrition services, WASH facilities, child protection and cover themes such as stereotypes defined by gender, economic status or social groups, existing biases, and barriers on the same) 

9. Please share some examples of some campaigns, messages or other programmes that were useful or not so useful for your needs. 

10. Based on the communication and messages received do you think that more girls from the village are continuing school, girl child is equally taken care as boy child, girls are not seen as burden etc. 

11.  Any area as a community you think should be taken up by health workers, community leaders, SHG groups etc. to enable you to access public goods and services? 

## Field-Level Observation Checklist 

|**On**|**Resources:**|
|---|---|
|1.|Availability of training material|
|2.|Availability of training modules|
|3.|Availability of training modules in local language(s)|
|4.|Availability of training records and attendance sheets|
|**5.**|Availability of training protocols pertaining to interpersonal interactions|
|**On**|**Physical environment:**|
|6.|Physical environment of training location(s) is well lit|
|7.|Physical environment of training location(s) is ventilated|
|8.|Availability of washroom facilities in training location(s)|
|9.|Availability of potable water in training location(s)|
|10.|Availability of seating area in the training location(s)|
|**On**|**Subjective Experience:**(Subjective experiences of SBCC related training conducted by Master Trainers with|
|Trainees of SBCC Cell is noted for observation)||
|11.|Do the trainees seem interested and engaged in the training?|
|12.|Do the trainees and trainers seem comfortable with each other?|
|13.|Do the trainees and trainers seem comfortable in the physical environment of the training location?|
|14.|Is the training interactive? Are there participatory training methodologies adopted for conducting training with|
||trainees?|
|15.|Was the ratio of trainer to trainees appropriate for increased knowledge acquisition and training delivery?|
|**On**|**Trainers’ training:**|
|16.|Have the trainers received training on how to conduct training?|
|17.|Have the trainers received training on the training modules to be used for the training?|
|18.|Have the trainers been sensitized in social and gender inequities?|
|19.|Have mixed-media tools been incorporated in training conducted?|
|**On**|**Reporting and monitoring:**|
|20.|Are the training details such as the number of participants documented?|
|21.|On Training effectiveness, how is the training effectiveness measured and monitored?|
|22.|How are the trainees’ learning levels and knowledge acquisition monitored?|
